## v2505.9 - Bounded Background Cognition Runtime Foundations Checkpoint

Adds message-independent cognition above the v2504.9 unified runtime without granting external autonomy. Background activation requires explicit enablement and is suppressed by foreground activity, quiet hours, sleeping state, elevated/critical resource pressure, open-cycle limits, minimum cadence, and an hourly cognitive budget. An admitted cycle opens exactly one existing unified cognitive work ticket. Provider-free structural cognition may complete bounded candidate-only memory, belief, goal, plan, self-model, conflict, or thought-continuation outcomes. Retained Era-6 scheduler tickets may bridge only prepared read-only inspection, memory-consolidation review, and plan-review evidence; provider-like proposal preparation or any ticket carrying execution authority is rejected. No background path contacts providers, browses, sends messages, executes tools, mutates source, infers approval, applies candidates, installs, promotes, or broadens operator authority.

## v2503.4.30 - Training Evidence Alpha III Provenance and Benchmark Execution

- Added approved-store dataset provenance certificates that re-read every manifest-bound approved record and verify exact digests before readiness can pass.
- Added operator-authorized frozen-corpus model execution with deterministic scoring receipts and no raw model-output persistence.
- Readiness now distinguishes a structurally valid manifest from a dataset proven to originate from the approved runtime store.
- No model training, provider promotion, or autonomous model selection authority was added.

## v2503.4.28.2 - Training Evidence Alpha II Triple-Audit Repair

- Re-audited v2503.4.28.1 adversarially and repaired three additional integrity gaps plus one scale blocker.
- Dataset manifests now validate semantic structure, uniqueness, record bindings, capability totals, runtime-only authority flags, and their digest; identical version retries restore instead of conflicting on creation time.
- Training approval now requires a verified chosen output. A failed attempt with an unverified claimed correction cannot be approved merely by accumulating quality points.
- Approved-record identity is immutable, and new manifest-bound SFT/preference exports verify every approved record digest before producing a versioned local dataset.
- Frozen evaluation corpora revalidate case uniqueness/completeness, scored evaluation receipts are tamper-evident, and readiness requires a valid complete baseline receipt bound to the exact corpus.
- Near-duplicate detection now uses bounded indexed token probes rather than all-pairs comparison; a 5,000-record distinct synthetic corpus completes in sub-second time in the audit environment while retained near-duplicate cases remain detected.
- No training, fine-tuning, distillation, provider selection, upload, or model promotion authority is granted.

# Eidolon Release History

## v2503.4.28.1 - Training Evidence Alpha II Audit Repair

- Repairs an unreachable default readiness threshold by replacing tiny per-capability dataset ceilings with scalable diversity ceilings.
- Binds dataset manifests to approved-record digests and validates manifest/corpus digests before readiness or scoring.
- Makes frozen evaluation corpora, evaluation results, and model-candidate identities fail closed on conflicting overwrite.
- Rejects duplicate evaluation result IDs and tightens comparison eligibility to complete same-corpus evaluations with no per-category governance regression.
- Grants no training, provider-selection, or model-promotion authority.

## v2503.4.28 - Training Evidence Alpha II Readiness Gate

- Adds a deterministic readiness gate requiring a versioned/diverse dataset, frozen evaluation corpus, and complete baseline before any future operator training review.
- Readiness never grants automatic training or model-promotion authority.

## v2503.4.27 - Governed Model Candidate Registry

- Adds a runtime-only, digest-bound registry for future model candidates; registration is not provider selection or promotion.

## v2503.4.26 - Model Comparison Reports

- Compares candidate and baseline evaluations only on the same frozen corpus, including quality and governance-violation deltas.

## v2503.4.25 - Baseline Model Benchmark Scoring

- Scores supplied complete holdout results with pass rate, violation counts, and latency without contacting or training a model; this is scoring scaffolding, not a model-execution runner.

## v2503.4.24 - Frozen Evaluation Corpus Framework

- Adds immutable-digest holdout corpora with unique case identities and capability/check contracts.

## v2503.4.23 - Versioned Balanced Dataset Manifests

- Adds deduplicated, per-capability-capped dataset manifests with stable version identities and SHA-256 manifests.

## v2503.4.22 - Training Quality Scoring v2

- Extends deterministic quality scoring with bounded difficulty and novelty signals while retaining explicit operator approval.

## v2503.4.21 - Verified Preference Pair Builder

- Derives chosen/rejected pairs only from sanitized correction records whose chosen output passed validation.

## v2503.4.20 - Expanded Training Privacy Sanitization

- Extends deterministic redaction to phone numbers, IPv4 addresses, URL credentials, and private-key blocks.

## v2503.4.19 - Training Deduplication and Diversity

- Adds exact/near duplicate rejection, frequency caps, and capability-distribution receipts.

## v2503.4.18 - Normalized Training Record Schema

- Adds a stable normalized view with capability labels, chosen/rejected outputs, difficulty, provenance, and semantic fingerprints.

## v2503.4.17 - Opt-In Training Workflow Capture

- Adds default-off training-evidence capture to isolated coding after deterministic verification and to bounded research after deterministic synthesis validation.
- Preserves failed-attempt to verified-correction pairs for software repair when available, while research capture binds the final synthesis to its validation result.
- Treats training capture as best-effort runtime evidence only; capture failure cannot change the underlying task result and grants no training or model-promotion authority.

## v2503.4.16 - Governed Model Training Evidence Foundation

- Adds explicit, runtime-only capture adapters for verified software development/repair and research synthesis outcomes without silently recording provider traffic.
- Keeps capture, dataset approval, model training, fine-tuning/distillation, and model promotion as separate authority boundaries.
- Preserves the existing v2503.5 research roadmap; this patch series does not replace that milestone.

## v2503.4.15 - Training Quality Gate and Dataset Export

- Scores sanitized candidate records from validation evidence, deterministic checks, correction pairs, and operator review.
- Requires explicit operator approval before a record enters the approved dataset set and separate explicit authorization before local JSONL export.
- Dataset approval/export never authorizes a training run or model promotion.

## v2503.4.14 - Training Privacy Sanitization

- Creates separately sealed sanitized copies of runtime training evidence while preserving the raw record for local audit.
- Redacts common secret-bearing keys, API/bearer tokens, private home paths, and email addresses before dataset eligibility.
- Requires later human/operator review; sanitization is not approval.

## v2503.4.13 - Runtime-Only Training Evidence Recorder

- Adds tamper-evident per-interaction training evidence under an explicit runtime root.
- Captures task type, input context, model output, validation evidence, optional corrected output, and provenance only after explicit capture authorization.
- Grants no provider, tool, source mutation, model training, distillation, or model-promotion authority.

## v2503.4.12 - Grounded Missing Anchor Repair

- Supplies missing-anchor retries with bounded nearby source neighborhoods selected from the exact disposable workspace.
- Allows the provider to correct only absent `old` anchors while retaining paths, intended `new` code, replacement order, and creates.
- Rejects scope or intent drift and preserves transient-candidate privacy and separate operator application authority.

## v2503.4.11 - Missing Anchor Classification

- Distinguishes replacement anchors that are absent from anchors that occur more than once.
- Routes missing anchors through grounded bounded regeneration instead of duplicate-occurrence selection.
- Retains rejected missing-anchor candidates only in transient memory and preserves separate application, installation, promotion, and release authority.

## v2503.4.10 - Anchor Intent Binding

- Keeps validated paths, old blocks, new blocks, and created files authoritative throughout duplicate-anchor repair.
- Accepts only bounded occurrence selections from the provider, preventing sequential replacement drift from erasing later duplicate context.
- Preserves transient-candidate privacy, focused verification, active-source immutability, and separate operator installation and promotion authority.

## v2503.4.9 - Provider Anchor Drift Recovery

- Restores exact `old` replacement blocks from the previously validated transient candidate before processing a provider's duplicate-anchor repair.
- Retains provider-supplied corrected `new` intent and occurrence choices while rejecting path, edit-count, or replacement-count scope drift.
- Keeps missing-occurrence recovery inside the same bounded cycle without granting source application, installation, promotion, or release authority.

## v2503.4.8 - Missing Anchor Selection Recovery

- Adds a selection-only fallback when an otherwise usable duplicate-anchor repair omits its required occurrence number.
- Binds selections to exact paths and replacement indexes before deterministic anchor expansion, rejecting missing, duplicate, invalid, or extra choices.
- Keeps repair candidates transient and allows one additional bounded repair call without expanding source-application, installation, promotion, or release authority.

## v2503.4.7 - Deterministic Duplicate Anchor Selection

- Changes the duplicate-anchor retry contract from model-copied source context to a bounded one-based occurrence selection.
- Deterministically widens the selected occurrence into one unique exact replacement while retaining unchanged surrounding text.
- Replays earlier replacements, rejects invalid selections, consumes the temporary occurrence marker, and preserves all source and authority boundaries.

## v2503.4.6 - Parser-Grounded Syntax Repair

- Reconstructs a rejected compact candidate only inside its disposable workspace and parses the complete resulting Python without executing it.
- Gives syntax retries the failing relative path, parser line and column, sanitized message, and a bounded numbered candidate excerpt.
- Preserves transient rejected output, same-path constraints, active-source immutability, and separate installation and promotion authority.

## v2503.4.5 - Sequential Replacement Context Repair

- Replays preceding unique replacements before locating duplicate anchors later in the same compact edit.
- Produces occurrence excerpts from the candidate's intermediate file state, matching the state that triggered validation.
- Falls back to the bounded full repair prompt when specialized duplicate context cannot be constructed, rather than stopping before provider contact.

## v2503.4.4 - Duplicate Replacement Anchor Repair

- Adds a dedicated correction prompt for `compact_replacement_not_unique` instead of discarding the rejected candidate and regenerating blindly.
- Supplies bounded excerpts around every duplicate occurrence so the provider can widen only the intended exact anchor.
- Keeps rejected provider output transient, preserves the same path and execution bindings, and verifies the repair through a complete one-command product-repair cycle.

## v2503.4.3 - Dedicated Isolated-Coding Syntax Repair

- Replaces the repeated full implementation prompt after `syntax_invalid` with a small syntax-only correction prompt bound to the same request, execution digest, attempt, and file paths.
- Carries the rejected candidate only in transient memory so the provider can correct the exact malformed edit without persisting provider output.
- Verifies an invalid-first, corrected-second provider sequence through the one-command product-repair cycle while preserving source immutability and separate application authority.
- Gives a safely stopped cycle a new request identity bound to the prior sealed-result lineage, while successful cycle replay remains exactly-once.

## v2503.4.2 - One-Command Supervised Product Repair Cycle

- Added one exact digest-bound product-repair cycle command that performs review, isolated preparation, execution authorization, provider generation, and bounded verification.
- Preserved separate application and installation authority; the combined cycle never mutates active source.
- Added sealed-result replay so repeated controls do not repeat provider or test work.
- Retained the individual review, prepare, and isolated-execution commands for stepwise operation and recovery.

## v2503.4.1 - Research-Finding Routing and Anti-Overfit Repair
- Classifies bounded-research, source-discovery, citation, evidence-matrix, and free-tier findings before generic conversation-quality matching.
- Uses bounded word and phrase boundaries so `repeated` no longer triggers the `repeat` conversation rule.
- Binds bounded-research findings to the research reasoning and autonomous-research owners with the retained v2503.2-v2503.4 suites.
- Tells the isolated coding provider that focused tests are verification evidence, not implementation templates, while retaining fail-closed fixture-literal leakage rejection.
- Preserves private finding text, source/runtime separation, provider neutrality, exact authorization, rollback, and operator installation/promotion authority.

## v2503.4 - Evidence-Language Consistency and Source-Quality Enforcement
- Rejects malformed public citation URLs before identity, matrix, history, export, or rendered-report admission.
- Assigns deterministic source-evidence roles and dimension-specific quality caps so implementation precedent, pricing evidence, first-party claims, primary data, independent analysis, community reports, and promotional summaries cannot be treated as interchangeable proof.
- Derives candidate evidence strength and rendered evidence summaries from the complete matrix instead of provider-generated confidence prose.
- Reserves `Most promising` for candidates that clear the moderate-confidence threshold; below-threshold selections are explicitly labeled `Best current lead for further research`.
- Deterministically corrects a valid but weaker model-selected candidate when another admitted candidate has a stronger matrix ranking.
- Adds 34 adversarial checks and retains all 57 v2503.3 source-independence checks with zero network, provider, external-action, or authority expansion.
- Preserves source/runtime separation, GET/HEAD-only research, exact authorization, configured-provider neutrality, long-response delivery, cancellation, exactly-once, rollback, and operator installation/promotion authority.

## v2503.3 - Source Independence and Recommendation Confidence Browser Candidate
- Adds deterministic provider-neutral canonical source identities that normalize harmless URL variation while distinguishing pages, hosts, publishers, mirrors, syndication, and repeated citations.
- Clusters evidence conservatively by auditable lineage signals; same-lineage repetition increases a visible repetition count but contributes only one independent confirmation, while ambiguous similarity remains explicitly uncertain.
- Extends candidate evidence assessment with content-free observed-citation, unique-source, independent-lineage, derivative/repetition, authoritative-source, freshness/quality, support/refute, and unresolved counts without allowing citation volume alone to raise confidence.
- Presents the retained three-candidate by four-dimension matrix with supported, weak, contradicted, researched-with-no-credible-evidence, and not-researched states plus compact independent-source strength.
- Calibrates recommendations as unsupported, tentative, moderate-confidence, or high-confidence from coverage, independent lineages, authority/quality/freshness, contradictions, and candidate specificity; an invalid-model deterministic recovery remains tentative by policy.
- Integrates source independence, matrix state, confidence reasons, history, comparison, and Markdown export into the existing research surfaces without adding a second dashboard or persisting raw objectives, queries, page bodies, credentials, cookies, private receipts, or provider payloads.
- Preserves v2503.2 matrix retries/gaps/candidate recovery, the 12,000-character research-response ceiling, 2,400-character ordinary governed-command ceiling, configured provider/model, exactly-once replay, GET/HEAD-only public research, and all operator authority boundaries.
- Adds focused deterministic v2503.3 regression coverage and retains v2502.8, v2503.0, v2503.1, v2503.2, replay-stress, verifier-hot-path, and long-response coverage. Browser verification performs no configured-provider or public-web contact.
- This checkpoint is an **unpromoted Browser candidate** for one later native Windows Codex engineering gate. It is not installed, promoted, certified, or released, and v2503.4 is not begun.

## Post-v2503.2 - Quick Verifier Parallel-Core Optimization
- Runs `python-compile`, `version-inventory`, `corrective-suite`, and `import-compatibility` concurrently in the quick profile because they are independent read-only gates.
- Preserves exact source-content hashing as each parallel stage completes plus final whole-tree immutability verification; full verification remains sequential.
- Repairs a policy/execution mismatch so the named v2502.9 integrated Desktop/research gate and current v2503.2 regression actually execute in quick.
- Keeps historical acceptance coverage in full/segmented verification and uses a measured 360-second cold-Windows quick target without changing release, installation, provider, or autonomy authority.

## v2503.2 - Candidate-Specific Evidence Follow-Up
- Adds a bounded two-pass comparative research workflow: broad configured-model discovery followed by deterministic candidate-specific evidence gathering and final synthesis.
- Plans at most four follow-up searches across demand, competition, implementation dependencies, and free-tier feasibility, prioritizing the recommendation and the weakest candidate evidence without exceeding the existing eight-query hard limit.
- Carries candidate and evidence-dimension lineage through transient observations so final claims can be checked against product-specific sources rather than generic market articles.
- Adds per-candidate evidence coverage and explicit remaining-gap reporting; missing dimensions remain visible instead of being smoothed into confidence.
- Keeps candidate names and query text out of public history receipts, preserving only digests, counts, dimension labels, phase status, and truthful two-pass provider-request counts.
- Retains one exact session authorization, GET/HEAD-only external access, configured-provider neutrality, transient raw content, exactly-once replay, and all operator authority boundaries.
- Adds a 25-check deterministic full-session regression suite and registers it once in the bounded quick release verifier.
- Post-v2503.2 source hygiene now completes active-surface consolidation: the repository root retains only `README.md`, `README_NEXT_STEPS.md`, and `README_RELEASE_HISTORY.md`; legacy path-sensitive checkpoint/handoff/validation/roadmap dependencies are redirected into `archive/docs/`; and unreferenced roadmap/ledger material leaves active `docs/`.
- Preserves every retired artifact byte-for-byte with old/new path, size, and SHA-256 evidence in `archive/docs/DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_MANIFEST.json`; `docs/README.md` indexes only active compatibility/reference surfaces.

## v2503.1 - Research Synthesis and Desktop Editing Repair
- Requires numbered opportunity research to return concrete, distinct products with an explicit customer, problem, product behavior, zero-budget rationale, evidence summary, citations, and uncertainty.
- Rejects generic strategy, guide, pricing, validation-framework, and idea-list rows at the deterministic validation boundary.
- Requires the comparative recommendation to select one admitted opportunity and overlap its observed evidence.
- Derives weak, moderate, or strong evidence labels from admitted source quality and independence; weak evidence renders only a tentative recommendation and is never silently called validated.
- Preserves truthful synthesis-provider contact and request counts in privacy-minimized historical reports without implying that history inspection itself contacted a provider.
- Adds a native Desktop right-click edit menu with Cut, Copy, Paste, and Select all for the composer and selectable conversation or activity output; write actions remain limited to the editable composer.
- Adds focused regression coverage for the failed live SaaS report shape, recommendation binding, evidence calibration, historical receipts, and Desktop mouse/keyboard context-menu access.
- Rebalances the bounded quick verifier by leaving redundant historical checkpoint wrappers to full/segmented verification while retaining current privacy, Desktop, research, compile, dashboard, integrity, and smoke coverage. The increasingly large runtime-status scan receives a bounded 90-second process margin without raising the nine-minute profile budget.

## v2502.7 - Source Strategy and Adaptive Follow-Up
- Prefers primary and authoritative source categories when appropriate while tracking domain duplication, diversity, independence, freshness, relevance, and quality.

- Stops on evidence, time, page, or source-failure thresholds and preserves exactly-once replay.
- Does not bypass paywalls/access controls, authenticate automatically, obey page instructions that expand authority, or weaken redirect/DNS/peer/credential/cookie/byte/timeout defenses.

- Decomposes broad research objectives into deterministic required facts, comparative criteria, assumptions, unknowns, and stopping conditions using the retained inquiry/reasoning architecture.

- Keeps planning provider-neutral and deterministic, and does not persist rejected objectives into a public research session.

- Excludes private objectives, search queries, raw pages, credentials, cookies, provider payloads, internal stack traces, private filesystem paths, and unrelated runtime records.

- Adds a production provider-neutral adapter for public search and public page observation under the v2501.0 bounded research lifecycle.

- Combines casual context with verified governed-action receipts and preserves action-only responses unchanged.
- Routes explicit repeated diagnostic commands while restoring the actual pre-action topic when the operator asks to return to it.
- Replaces the unbounded monolithic full verifier with a current representative profile; broad historical coverage remains resumable through `tools/segmented_release_verify.py`.
- v2503.2 verifier-profile cleanup narrows routine `quick` execution to 10 high-signal stage names, preserves the retired acceptance gates in `full`, removes the unused legacy quick-stage inventory, and tightens the quick performance target from 540 seconds to a measured 360-second cold-Windows boundary without deleting historical certification coverage.
- Skips the expensive legacy-data inventory on ordinary launch when a populated external runtime already exists and no interrupted migration journal needs reconciliation.
- Reconciles the quick verifier's Windows wall-clock budget with observed clean-host variance while keeping functional failures independent from timing evidence.
- Passed 106 focused v1500.9 checks, the 15-step quick Windows profile in 420.469 seconds, and the 21-step full Windows profile in 482.959 seconds before packaging.

## v1500.8.0 Conversational Target Continuity and Repetition Resistance Checkpoint
- Makes the newest user message the explicit response target in streaming and non-streaming conversation.

- Gives explicit corrections and topic changes structural priority over stale prior requests.
- Removes repeated sentences and strongly paraphrased whole-answer repeats without another provider request.

- Adds the focused `tools/v1500_8_conversation_target_continuity_tests.py` suite.

- Made newer durable single-value family roles supersede stale conflicting edges across restart while preserving correction lineage.
- Added inverse and directional kinship responses that keep the requested subject and object roles intact.
- Relevance-gated active facts, family edges, and project associations before provider prompting, omitting unrelated personal context from casual turns.
- Added a 26-check natural-language, correction, relevance, privacy, and authority-neutrality suite.

- **v1489.0091-v1489.0100:** added non-mutating configured-provider validation, precise failure/recovery classification, draft and operation preservation through provider loss, no automatic switch/replay behavior, stream-chunk hardening, settings persistence checks, and redacted provider error surfaces.

## v1321.9 Candidate Approaches
Completed the first deliberative-planning arc. Goal and project-understanding digests now produce multiple viable approaches only when consequential tradeoffs are present; reversible low-risk routine decisions collapse to one bounded default. Records are source-manifest bound, tamper-evident, content-minimized, and non-executing.



## v1322.9 Tradeoff Evaluation
Completed calibrated seven-criterion comparison of candidate approaches with evidence confidence, explicit unknowns, tie preservation, deterministic weighting, sealed records, ordinary-chat integration, and no conversion of a recommendation into execution authority.



## v1323.9 Assumption Ledger
Added durable planning assumptions with evidence digests, confidence, validation methods, invalidation conditions, revision lineage, manifest freshness, tamper rejection, ordinary-chat inspection, and no authority conversion.



## v1324.9 Plan Construction
Added ordered acyclic plan construction with dependency, checkpoint, verification, rollback, acceptance-criterion, and stop-condition lineage. Tradeoff ties block silent selection; structurally executable plans remain non-executing and non-authorizing.



## v1325.9 Plan Critique
Added a separate immutable plan critique across missing requirements, hidden coupling, unsafe authority, and weak verification, with impact/assumption evidence, blocking severity, ordinary-chat inspection, and no plan mutation or execution authority.

## v1326.9 Risk-Sensitive Planning
Scaled isolation, review depth, verification breadth, and approval requirements from actual plan mutations, predicted blast radius, migrations, unknown paths, protected authority surfaces, and critique severity. Requirements remain explicit constraints rather than grants; critical protected-surface plans fail closed.

## v1327.9 Dynamic Replanning
Added evidence-triggered replanning that preserves completed-step evidence, revises only unfinished work, binds source-manifest changes and evidence digests, explains route changes, rejects authority-bearing plans, and retains the route when no material evidence changed.

## v1328.9 Stop and Escalation Logic
Added deterministic stop behavior for repeated failure, material uncertainty, boundary conflict, resource exhaustion, unsafe side effects, and protected surfaces. Stops produce deduplicated actionable choices but never auto-retry, auto-resume, execute a choice, or manufacture authority.

## v1329.9 Plan Quality Scoring
Added outcome-grounded scoring for prediction accuracy, rework efficiency, step necessity, dependency coverage, and acceptance coverage. Scores require observed verification evidence; missing evidence remains not evaluable rather than self-scored.

## v1330.9 Deliberative-Planning Checkpoint
Completed Phase 3 by integrating candidate generation, tradeoff evaluation, assumption lineage, executable-but-unauthorized plan construction, independent critique, risk-sensitive safeguards, evidence-triggered replanning, stop/escalation logic, and outcome-grounded quality scoring. Ties, blockers, and missing evidence remain explicit rather than silently resolved.

Added content-minimized contracts for file read/patch, search, Git, shell, browser, build, test, and service tool classes with explicit schemas, side-effect classes, authority classes, and evidence-bound availability states. Registry construction performs no tool probe or invocation.

## v1489 Conversation Repair Candidate (Unpromoted, Revised)

The first human daily-use gate remains failed; private trial evidence stays outside source-only release material. Desktop Codex native Windows review found that the first repair still produced an approximately 2,240-token ordinary provider prompt and generic help/intake behavior despite correct desktop SSE transport. The revised candidate keeps the successful governed SSE, accepted-operation identity, no-automatic-replay, explicit cancellation, viewer-detach, reconciliation, response-field, AI-generation, timeout, and Ollama keep-alive work intact.

Ordinary non-operator conversation now selects one bounded `casual_fast` provider lane. Fresh turns have a 600 estimated-token ceiling; recent-continuity and meaningful emotional/relational turns have a 900 estimated-token ceiling. Provider prompts contain compact identity/safety and casual behavior contracts, relevant explicit relationship/preference cues when available, at most two recent completed turns selected by the existing topic machinery, authoritative follow-up/repetition consequences, and the latest message. Cognitive architecture, operational, planning, campaign, project, task, release, action, and self-maintenance projections remain internally computed/governed where applicable but are not serialized merely because they exist. Content-free prompt metrics expose lane, estimate, budget, included/omitted categories, and bound result. Operator and inherited operator-thread turns retain the existing governed prompt path.

Deterministic fixtures cover the casual token ceilings, current-subject preservation, meaningful acknowledgement guidance, repetition suppression in both streaming and non-streaming provider prompts, operator-lane separation, explicit preference continuity, desktop transport/exactly-once behavior, privacy, and source-only packaging. A later native Windows check exposed persistent interview-like question pressure even when `maximum_follow_up_questions` was zero. This revision recognizes explicit sharing/closure cues, counts repeated recent assistant question endings, distinguishes `none`, required-clarification, user-invited, and bounded optional question permission, and enforces the resulting budget on actual provider output. Non-streaming output is finalized before commit; streaming buffers only the current punctuation unit so disallowed question sentences are never shown and then retracted. The guard makes no retry or second provider request and is bypassed for governed operator prompts.

The strengthened v1489 fixtures cover direct sharing, closure, repeated question-ending suppression, several consecutive question-free replies, required clarification, user-invited questions, streaming/non-streaming parity, no corrective stream replacement, one provider request, one committed turn, and preservation of the existing desktop transport repairs. The candidate still cannot score or certify itself. Runtime/release authority remains at v1488.9. Native response-quality and cold/warm first-visible timing acceptance must be re-run on Desktop Windows with the configured provider before operator review and a second unscripted human daily-use trial; v1490 remains blocked.

### Trial 2 grounding and command-routing repair (still unpromoted)

A subsequent Windows daily-use trial found that an immediately preceding user correction could be lost before provider prompt assembly and that a compound read-only maintenance command could be misrouted into ordinary generation. The repair now protects attributable immediate user evidence before topic segmentation, keeps user evidence explicitly distinct from system/personality/policy guidance, and uses provider-free deterministic responses when an immediate correction is fully attributable or a historical-memory request has no attributable evidence.

Clear supported low-risk operator commands now route through the existing governed action system before conversational generation. A content-free execution-truth receipt binds action ID, capability, persisted status, completion state, attempt count, state digest, and redacted result-evidence digest. Success language is emitted only from a valid completed receipt; missing, pending, blocked, failed, cancelled, timed-out, or tampered receipt states remain visibly non-successful. Reconnect/replay uses the persisted action state and does not create a second execution attempt. Ordinary wishes and hypotheticals remain conversation-only.

Focused Trial 2 fixtures use synthetic wording only and retain the v1489 casual-fast budgets, question-pressure repair, desktop SSE, exactly-once, approval, privacy, and source-only boundaries. Native Ollama was unavailable in the repair environment, so Windows native replay and timing evidence remain outstanding. Runtime/release authority remains v1488.9; this is not a promoted v1489 release and v1490 remains blocked.

Desktop Windows review found one blocking overcorrection before that trial: low continuity confidence on a fresh session caused an explicit invitation to lose its one-question allowance, and the output gate removed the provider's generated question. The repaired candidate separates low-confidence continuity from unsafe evidence, preserves explicit invitations and genuinely required clarification when evidence is otherwise structurally valid, and replaces broad `ask me` substring matching with anchored invitation forms. Complaints and negated wording no longer grant permission. Focused verification increased to 23 cases, and native Ollama checks confirmed question-free sharing, one retained invited question, question-free complaint handling, exactly one provider request/completion, and no replacement event. The artifact remains unpromoted and still requires the second unscripted human daily-use trial.

### Trial 3 compound grounding and memory-truth repair (still unpromoted)

A third Windows daily-use trial exposed two remaining trust defects. Compound current messages could place a correction before a request to repeat that correction and explain the proper uncertainty behavior, yet deterministic grounding still resolved the recall against the previous completed user turn. Separately, weak semantic association and assistant-authored capability material could be treated as support for a fluent historical narrative about shared difficulties.

The bounded repair now extracts current-message correction evidence before previous-turn recall, excludes the recall instruction itself, preserves named details and negation, and composes the requested correction plus uncertainty explanation when both are deterministically attributable. Historical-memory truth now evaluates provenance before provider generation: attributable user-authored records can support bounded claims, while assistant-authored material, non-assertive user questions/hypotheticals, weak matches, malformed or unattributed records, and unresolved conflicting evidence cannot. Fully determined grounding and unsupported-history cases bypass the provider; public diagnostics record only content-free state, counts, provenance classes, match strength, and uncertainty requirement.

Focused Trial 3 fixtures use synthetic content and preserve Trial 2 governed action routing, receipt-bound execution truth, exactly-once request/action/turn behavior, desktop streaming, approval controls, privacy, rollback, and source-only boundaries. Native Ollama was unavailable in the repair environment, so Windows native replay and redacted first-visible/total timing evidence remain required. Archived heading: v1152.0-v1152.2 Belief Revision and Uncertainty Foundations
- v1152.0 converts evidence-grounded ordinary-turn reflections into provisional belief candidates with stable subject identity and explicit evidence lineage.
- v1152.1 classifies uncertainty as low, material, high, or insufficient-evidence and preserves confidence separately from uncertainty.
- v1152.2 stores one idempotent belief-candidate phase behind authoritative conversation completion; candidates may inform later conversation but cannot approve, authorize, or execute actions.


Archived heading: v1152.3-v1152.5 Belief Revision Integration
- **v1152.3:** atomically integrates evidence-grounded ordinary-turn belief candidates into the existing durable belief-revision ledger with stable candidate lineage and idempotent event handling.
- **v1152.4:** merges supporting evidence into matching beliefs and creates explicit conflict sets for competing same-subject propositions; unresolved conflicts remain visible and non-authorizing.
- **v1152.5:** applies explicit corrections as historical supersession and emits a bounded `belief_revision` projection for ordinary conversation. Contested and superseded beliefs are withheld from prompt reuse, and no belief can approve or execute action.

Archived heading: v1152.6-v1152.8 Belief Revision Reliability and Deliberation

- **v1152.6:** hardens evidence retraction so repeated correction events are idempotent, historical evidence remains auditable, and affected conflicts require review rather than silent automatic resolution.
- **v1152.7:** adds a read-only bounded conflict-deliberation projection that can influence ordinary conversation while preserving uncertainty, withholding resolution authority, and contacting no provider.
- **v1152.8:** quarantines malformed conflict structures, keeps deliberation prompt-safe and bounded, and recovers partial conversation completion after durable belief integration without duplicating beliefs or projections.

Validation: focused and retained cognitive tests, legacy belief-store compatibility, privacy/source-only boundaries, fresh-extract compilation, source immutability, and archive inspection. No installation, promotion, certification, model replacement, or action-authority expansion occurred.

Archived heading: v1152.9 Belief Revision Alpha Read-Only Checkpoint

Consolidated v1152.0-v1152.8 into one executable, strictly read-only checkpoint covering provisional belief formation, evidence lineage, explicit uncertainty, durable integration, supporting-evidence merge, competing-proposition conflict sets, correction supersession, historical preservation, idempotent evidence retraction, bounded conflict deliberation, malformed-state quarantine, and partial-integration recovery.

The checkpoint exposes structural counts, booleans, bounds, and digests only. It does not expose propositions, evidence references, conflict identifiers, conversations, prompts, provider payloads, private memory text, or hidden reasoning. It cannot resolve conflicts, mutate beliefs, contact providers, execute commands or actions, create approvals or authorizations, install or replace models, package, promote, certify, or prove consciousness.

A read-only defect was repaired in the shared metadata-lock dependency: importing belief-revision modules previously imported `paths.py`, whose module-level initialization created the configured runtime directory. Metadata lock roots are now resolved lazily, so read-only belief imports and the v1152.9 CLI leave a missing runtime absent. Mutation paths still create lock directories only when a write is actually requested.

The checkpoint also exposed a durable-state inconsistency in evidence retraction. Confidence recomputation could relabel a belief `active` even though it remained inside an unresolved active conflict. The retraction path now restores `contested` for nonterminal conflict members, preserves the conflict for review, and still performs no automatic resolution.

Validation includes the focused v1152.9 checkpoint suite, all retained v1152 belief and v1151 reflection suites, v1150 cognitive-backbone and conversation-recovery suites, legacy belief-store compatibility, source-only and privacy checks, complete registry invocation, fresh-extract compilation, source immutability, and archive inspection. Native Desktop verification, optional-provider certification, installation, promotion, and release certification remain pending.

Archived heading: v1205.9 General Small-Project Implementation Checkpoint

Seals a content-free seven-stage checkpoint for the consolidated website, JavaScript, and Python small-project implementation coordinator. The checkpoint binds the exact proposal revision, exactly-once approval receipt, grounded plan, capability-registry owner, durable coordinator journal, delegated implementation result, and operator-review authority boundary. The checkpoint also includes a direct ordinary-chat command-distinction audit. Standalone casual conversation, wishes, hypotheticals, quotations, and suggestions remained non-actionable, while a direct imperative created one persistent approval-gated proposal. The mixed example received a conversational response but created zero proposals. The exact deficiency is `mixed_turn_whole_message_classification_drops_embedded_action_clause`.

This capability is therefore **not certified complete** at v1205.9. The first implementation bundle after the checkpoint is **v1206.0-v1206.2 Natural Conversation and Command Distinction**. No clause-level implementation was added during the checkpoint because the missing behavior requires a broader conversation/action separation design rather than a narrow defect repair.

No apply, rollback, repair, dependency-installation, network, model-management, source-modification, release, certification, promotion, or independent authority was added.

Archived heading: v1208.9.1 Desktop Repair Checkpoint

Repaired defects found during Windows review of the uninstalled v1208.9 candidate. Browser tests now navigate the real loopback-served entrypoint and require its runtime marker. Node tests detect concurrency-flag support and guard writes outside private scratch space. Python tests prefer the active environment, use an audit hook to block the reproduced `pathlib` escape, and expose unavailable pytest deterministically. Both language-runtime guards identify that they are not OS containers. Windows lock contention now retries permission races for a bounded 60 seconds, and checkpoint fixtures use explicit UTF-8. Same-sentence wish-plus-command turns preserve conversation while extracting one supervised action. Playwright and pytest requirements are declared separately, and each repaired defect has deterministic regression coverage.

This remains an uninstalled development candidate. It grants no automatic repair, apply, model-management, release, promotion, or independent authority.

### v1490.2 revised unpromoted Desktop-findings repair
Desktop Codex invalidated the original v1490.2 review claim by reproducing Windows source-immutability failure and identifying protected-authority tokenization, cross-module test-attribution, and cold-performance defects. The revised candidate keeps Bundle A read-only and provider-free while switching source immutability to raw-byte digests, attributing test evidence conservatively by imported source module/symbol, preserving compound authority identifiers and called protected dependencies, and caching only process-local structural/index evidence with source/test add/change/delete invalidation. The corrected evidence model no longer treats unrelated same-name tests as coverage for `self_maintenance.py`; unsupported focus families are rejected honestly. Current browser/container verification is evidence for re-review, not promotion or certification. Bundle B remains blocked until Desktop Codex/native Windows review.

## v2503.2 verifier hot-path optimization follow-up

Optimized verifier internals without broadening authority or reducing certification coverage. Import-compatibility cases remain isolated processes but can execute concurrently; quick verification throttles nested compile/import worker counts to avoid oversubscription; disposable compilation supports bounded worker configuration; and corrective synthetic evidence validation reuses one already measured source digest rather than repeatedly hashing an unchanged tree. Added `tools/v2503_2_verifier_hot_path_optimization_tests.py` to preserve concurrency, deterministic ordering, worker-throttling, and precomputed-source-digest contracts. Native Windows review measured clean functional runs at 265.485, 278.235, and 318.517 seconds under changing host load; the candidate's unverified 240-second threshold was repaired to a 360-second cold-Windows target.
